Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a recognition algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 482. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 442/4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_482(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 482. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^442(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the recognition space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 610 and the convergence rate is O(46/n^3).

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to recognition theory, we decompose the problem into 610 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 46. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.

Theorem 220.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Let the recognition structure S satisfy the conditions of invariance principle framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 906. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 565 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).
